0 [el F

PROGRAMMABLE REGISTERS

As indicated above, the microprocessor 6809 has three additional registers compared to 6800. These registers are: a direct page register (DP), a user stack pointer register (U) and a second index register (Y).

ACCUMULATEURS (A, B, D)

Registers A and B are universal accumulators used for arithmetic calculations and data manipulation. Some instructions concatenate registers A and B to form a single 16-bit accumulator. Register A is the heavy weight byte of this battery referenced register D.

DIRECT PAGE REGISTER (DP)

The direct page register of circuit 6809 is used to extend the possibilities of addressing in direct mode. The contents of this register appear at strong weight addresses (A8-A15) during the execution of a direct address instruction. This register allows the direct address mode, under program control, to be used throughout the address space. To allow compatibility with the 6800 family, all bits in this register are zeroed to the processor initialization.

INDEX REGISTERS (X, Y)

Index registers are used for indexed address modes. When calculating the actual address, the 16 bits of this register are used.

159


===== Page 158 =====
read. The addresses contained in these registers can be used as a data pointer and modified by an optional constant or a displacement value. When storing data in table form, in some indexed address modes, the contents of the index registers are incremented or decremented to point to the next element. The four registers (X, Y, U, S) can be used as index registers.

BATTERY POINTERS (U,S)

The stack pointer (S) is used automatically by the processor to memorize machine states during the execution of subprograms and interruptions. The 6809 pointers point the top of the stack, opposite the 6800 pointer, which pointed the next free spot on the pile. The user stack pointer (U) is controlled by the programmer exclusively, allowing the passage of parameters from and to sub-programs with ease. U and S stack pointers have the same possibilities as X and Y registers for indexed address modes and for stacking/skinning instructions. The 6809 micro processor can be used as a processor with battery management, allowing the use of high-level language and modular programming methods.

PROGRAM COUNTER (PC)

The program counter is used by the processor to point the address of the next instruction to be executed. Relative address allows the program counter to be used as an index register in some cases.

CONDITION CODE REGISTER (CC)
The condition code log defines the processor state at any time.

GHDMEEN

I flerenve

- What?

Zeru Neqatit Interruption mask IAQ

DESCRIPTION OF THE REGISTER
CONDITION CODES (CC)

BIT 0 (C)

The bit 0 is the restraint indicator, it usually indicates the retention during an operation of the arithmetic and logical unit. C is also used to represent withholding on subtraction instructions (CMP, NEG, SUB, SBC). In this case this indicator is the complement to the retention during a logical and arithmetic unit operation.

BIT 1 (V)

Bit 1 is the overflow indicator; it is put to one if there is overflow, in addition to two signed after an arithmetic operation. The overflow is detected during an operation in the logical and arithmetic unit when the MSB restraint does not match the MSB-! restraint.

BIT 2 (2)
Bit 2 is the zero indicator bit, it is set to one if the result of the previous operation is zero.

BIT 3 (N)

Bit 3 indicates a negative result, it contains exactly the value of the high weight bit of the byte resulting from the previous operation. A negative result, in addition to two, positions N to 1.

BIT 4 (1)

Bit 4 is the mask bit of IRQ interruptions. This bit set to one, the processor will not take into account interruptions arriving on the IRQ line. NMI, FIRQ, IRQ, RESET and SWI all position 1 to 1. SWI 2 and SWI 3 do not affect !.

BIT 5 (H)

Bit 5 is the half holding bit; it is used to indicate a hold tdu bi/ 3 as a result of an 8-bit addition only (ADC or ADD). This bit is used in a DAA statement to perform a decimal adjustment operation. The status of this indicator is indefinite in all subtraction instructions or equivalent.

161


===== Page 160 =====
BIT 6 (F)

Bit 6 is the mask bit of FIRQ rapid interruptions. The processor will not take into account the interruptions of the FIRQ line, when this bit is one. NMI, FIRQ, SWI, and RESET all position F a one. F is not affected by IRQ, SWI 2 and SWI 3.

BIT 7 (E)

The bit E is the backup status indicator bit; It indicates that the complete machine condition (all registers) is stacked instead of the previous state (PC and CC). The stacked CC registry bit E is used on an interruption return (ITR) to determine the extent of removal. Therefore, the current bit E left in the CC register represents the previous action.

ADDRESSING MODES

The basic instructions of any computer are particularly improved by the presence of powerful address modes. The available address mode set of 6809 is currently the most powerful of existing microprocessors. For example, 6809 has 59 basic instructions, but it allows for 1464 different possibilities of instructions and address modes. New address modes allow modern programming techniques. The following address modes are available in 6809: Inherent or implicit (Includes accumulators) Intermediate Extended Indirect Scope Direct Indexed Registry

Zero displacement

Constant displacement

Accumulator displacement

Self-incrementation/decrementation

Indirect index

Long/short relative connection

Addressing relative counter program

162


===== Page 161 =====
INHERENT (INCLUDING ACCUMULATORS)

In this address mode, the instruction operation code contains all the necessary address information. Examples of inherent address are: ABX, DAA, SWI, ASRA, CLRB, etc.

IMMEDIATE ATTENTION

In immediate address, the actual data address is located at the location immediately following the operation code; the data to be used as an address in the 6809 statement use two immediate values 8 and 16 bits depending on the size of the operand specified by the operation code. Examples of instructions using immediate address are:

LDA # $20
LDX # $F000
LDY # ASTER

Note: # means immediate address, $ means hexadecimal value.

EXTENDED APPROACH

In extended address, the content of the two bytes immediately following the operation code fully specifies the actual 16-bit address used by the instruction.

Note that the address generated by an extended instruction defines an absolute address and is not transferable. Examples of extended address include:

LDA ASTER
STX OBEL
LDD $2000

INDIRECT EXTENT
As a special case of indexed address (discussed below), an indirection level can be added to the extended address. In indirect extended mode, the two bytes following the post byte of an indexed instruction contain the address of the data address.

LDA [ASTER]

LDX ($ FFFE]

STU (OBEL]

163


===== Page 162 =====
DIRECT ADDRESS

Direct address is similar to extended address except that a byte of address only follows the operation code. This byte specifies the 8 bits low weight address to use. The 8-bit high weight address is provided by the direct page register. One byte of address is only required in direct address, so this mode requires less memory and runs faster than in extended address. Of course, only 256 locations (one page) can be defined without having to reposition the contents of the DP registry. Since the DP register is set to $ 00 at initialization, direct address on 6809 is compatible with direct address on 6800. Indirection is not permitted in direct address.

Examples of direct address include:

LDA $30
SETDP $10                ; (Assembly Directive)
LDB $1030

LDD <ASTER
Note: < is an assembly directive that forces direct address.

REGISTRY ADDRESSING

Some operation codes are followed by a byte that defines a registry or set of records to be used by the instruction, this byte is called POST OCTET.

Some examples of registry address are:

TFR X,Y                  ; Transfer of X to Y

EXG A,B,                 ; Exchange A and B

PSHS A,B,X,Y             ; Transfer dens S, Y, X, B and A

PULU X,Y,D               ; Transfer from U, D, X, then Y

ADDRESSAGE INDEXE

In any indexed address, one of the pointer registers (X, Y, U, S and sometimes PC) is used in calculating the actual address of the operator to be used by the instruction. Five basic indexing types are available and are described below. The post byte of an indexed instruction specifies the basic type and choice of address mode and the pointer register to be used. The table below shows the formats allowed for the post byte. The table ‘MODES d-Adressage INDEXED' gives the assembly form and number of cycles and d-bytes added to the indexed address base values for each variant.

Indexed — Zero displacement. In this mode, the selected pointer register contains the actual address of the data to be used by the statement. This mode is the fastest indexed mode.

Examples:
LDD 0,X
LDA 0,S

Indexed — Constant displacement. In this mode, a move in addition to two and the contents of one of the pointer registers are added to form the effective address of the operand. The initial content of the pointer register is not changed by addition. Three displacement values are available.

+ 4-bit(— 16 à +15)

+7-bit(— 128 à +127)

+ 15-bit(— 32 768 à +32 767)

The 5-bit displacement in addition to two is included in the post byte and therefore optimizes the use of bytes and cycles. The 8-bit displacement in addition to two is contained in a single byte following the post byte. 16-bit displacement in addition to

===== Page 165 =====
INDEXED ADDRESS MODES

Indexed — Accumulator displacement. This mode is similar to the indexed constant-moving mode, except that the value in addition to two in one of the accumulators (A, B or D) and the content of one of the pointer registers are added to form the effective address of the operand. The contents of the pointer register and the accumulator remain unchanged by addition. The post byte specifies the accumulator to use as a displacement and no additional byte is required. The advantage of an accumulator displacement is that the value of the displacement can be calculated by program in progress.

Examples:

LDA 8B,Y

LDX D,Y

LEAX B, X

Indexed — Auto Incrementation/Decrementation. In auto increment mode, the pointer register contains the operand address. Thus, after being used the pointer register is incremented by one or two. This address mode is very useful when using tables, moving data, or creating software batteries. In self decrementation the pointer register is decremented before being used as the data address. The use in self-decrementation is similar to that in self-incrementation, but the tables are scanned, the addresses elevated to weak addresses. The value of uncrement/decrement can be equal to 4 one or two to allow access to 8 or 16 bit data tables, it is selected by the programmer. The pre-decrement, post-increment aspect allows these modes used to create additional software batteries that behave in the same way as U and S batteries.

Examples of self-increment/decrement address modes include:

LDA X+
STD N++
LDB WY
LDX 1— —X

167


===== Page 166 =====
INDIRECT INDEX
All indirect indexed modes are included with the exception of incrementation/decrementation by one, or displacement of +4 bits and may have a specified additional direction level. In indirect address, the actual address is contained in the location specified by the contents of the index register, plus any displacement. In the example below, Accumulator A is loaded indirectly using an effective address calculated from the index and displacement register.

Before execution:

A=xx(indifferent)

X= $F000
$0100 LDA [10,X] the EA is then $F010 $F010 $F1 F150 is then the new $F011 $50 effective address
$F150 $AA

Implementation:

A=$AA Data loaded Note: EA = effective address. All indirect indexed modes are included except those that are meaningless (e.g. self increment/decrement by 1

indirect). Some examples of indirect indexed mode are:
LDA [,X]

LDD [10,X]
LDA [B,Y]
LDD CX+ +]

RELATIVE ATTENTION

The following byte(s) is treated as a signed displacement that is added to the program counter.

If the connection condition is true, then the calculated address (PC + signed displacement) is loaded into the program counter. Program execution continues to the new location as indicated by the PC, the short (1 byte) and long (two byte) relative address modes are available. Any memory location can be reached in long relative address mode, the effective address being interpreted modulo 216, Some examples of relative address are:

168


===== Page 167 =====
BEQ ASTER (short)

BGT OBEL (short)
ASTER LBEQ BAMBI         ; (long)
OBEL LBGT BUNNY          ; (long)
BAMBI NOP
BUNNY NOP

The program counter can be used as a pointer register with 8 or 16 bit signed moves. As in relative address the displacement is added to the current PC to form the actual address. The effective address is then used as an operand or given address. Relative address by program counter is used to write translatable programs. The tables relating to 4 a particular program keep the same link after translation of the program, if they are referenced relative to the program counter.

Examples:
LDA BUNNY, PCR
LEAX TABLE, PCR

Since the relative program counter mode is an indexing type, an additional level of indirection is usable.

LDA [ASTER,PCR]

LDU [OBEL,PCR]

JEU D’INSTRUCTIONS DU 6809

The 6809 instruction set is comparable to the 6800 and compatible with the source code. The number of operating codes has been reduced from 72 to 59, but thanks to its improved architecture and additional address modes, the number of operating codes available (with different address modes) is

from 197 to 1,464. Some instructions and address modes are described in

details below:

PSHU/PSHS

These instructions have the property of stacking any register(s) of the MPU either on the hardware stack (S) or on the user stack (U) in one instruction.

149


===== Page 168 =====
PULU/PULS

The removal instructions have the same property as the stacking instructions, in reverse order. The immediate byte according to the operation code of the stacking or depilation instructions determines which register(s) should be stacked or depilated. The actual stacking/skinning sequence is fixed; each bit determines a unique register to stack/depilate as indicated.

POST BYTE OF STACKING/SPILING
" — order of stacking removal order "
PC OR Y XK DP B A CC PSHS/PULS

FFFF... « growing memory address... 0000
PC S Y XxX DP B A CC PSHU/PULU

TFR/EXG

In 6809 each register can be transferred or exchanged with another register of the same format, i.e. 8 bits with 8 bits or 16 bits with 16 bits. The 4-7 bits of the post byte define the source register, while the 0-3 bits represent the destination register.

This is as follows:

0000 —- D 0101—PC

0001-X 1000-A

0010—Y 1001 -B

0011-U 1010 -CC

0100-S 1011 -DP

Note: All other combinations are indefinite and invalid.

Loading effective address (LEA)

The LEA statement runs by calculating the actual address used in an indexed statement and memorizes this address value, instead of the data in that address, in a pointer register. This makes all the hardware internal address features available to the programmer. Some of the implications of this instruction are illustrated with examples.

The LEA statement also allows the user to access data regardless of location. For example:

170


===== Page 169 =====
LEAX MSG1,PCR
LBSR PDATA (mail printing program)
MSG1 FCC IMESSAGE/

This set of programs prints ‘‘message'. By writing MSGI, PCR, the assembler calculates the distance between the present address and MSGI. This result is placed as a constant in the LEAX statement which is indexed by the value of the PC at the time of execution. No matter the position of the code during its execution, since the displacement calculated from the PC will put the absolute address of MSGI in the X pointer register. This code is completely translatable.

MUL

Multiply unsigned binary numbers of A and B accumulators and place the unsigned result in the 16-bit D accumulator.

LONG AND SHORT RELATIVE CONNECTIONS

The 6809 has the possibility to make connections related to the program counter over all memory space. In this mode, in case of connection, the signed displacement of 8 or 16 bits is added to the value of the program counter used as the effective address. This allows the program to connect anywhere in the 64 K memory space. The translatable code can be easily generated by using the relative connection. Both short (8-bit) and long (16-bit) connections are available.

APPENDIX C
ERROR MESSAGES

Input/output errors

- Bad Parameters The command parameter is incorrect. (Error in disk number).

- "Disk not ready" The disc is not ready.

- "Bad file descriptor" Syntax error in file descriptor or descriptor missing.

- "I/O Error" Physical impossibility of making an input/output.

- "File Format Error"

The file cannot be loaded. There are two possible causes: - error on diskette,

- the method of loading used is incorrect.

- "File not Found" The file described by the descriptor is not found on the specified device.

- "Verification Error"

The contents of the memory are different from the contents of the specified file.

- Bad Memory

The address indicated for setting up the object code is incorrect.

- "File Already Exists" The file name cannot be replaced by a name already existing in the catalogue (RENAME).

- "File Already Open" The file cannot be copied to itself.

183


===== Page 181 =====
- "Disk Write Protected" Writing test on a protected diskette.

- "Out Of Memory" There is not enough free RAM.

- "Disk Full" There is no more space on the diskette.

- Unreadable Disk The diskette used was not formatted.

- "Directory Full" There is no more space in the diskette catalogue.

Assembling errors

- Branch Out Of Range The address of the connection is too far from the address of the current instruction.

- "Bad Address" The address indicated for setting up the object code when assembling in memory is incorrect.

- "Bad Operator" The operator is not recognized by the assembler.

- "Bad Included" The file specified by INCLUD is in the cassette or contains another INCLUD directive.

- "Expression Error" Bad expression used in the field operand.

- Bad Opcode Bad operation code.

- "Undefined symbol" The symbol used has not been defined.

- "Missing END Statement" The source program is not completed with the END directive.

184


===== Page 182 =====
- "Multiply defined symbol" The same symbol is used several times.

- Register Error The symbol used does not correspond to a register of the microprocessor.

- Bad Label Bad label.

- Operand Too Large Operand too large.

- "DP Error" Error on direct page.

- "Symbol Full Table" There is no more room in the symbol table.

Other possible errors under editor-monitor or Entries/Releases

- Bad Command The command is not recognized by the system.

- " Bad Parameter(s)" The command parameter is incorrect or absent.

- "Missing operator" There is at least one parameter missing in SAVE and VERIFY commands under monitor control.

- "Undefined Symbol" The symbol used is not recognized by the monitor.

- "string not found" The string was not found.

- Internal Error *.

- "Missing information" There is missing information in the order.

185


===== Page 183 =====
- "Can-t Continue" The current program is stopped and can no longer be taken over by C (CONTINUE) because it is in ROM.

- "Bad Breakpoint" Error in command of the drop points.

*N.B.: This error is usually due to uncontrolled effects of the user program. Once the program is saved, it can be: be careful to reset the entire system by cutting off the power supply because Assembly-specific records may have been altered.

APPENDIX D
LA REPRÉSENTATION
FLOATING POINT

The dynamics of numbers represented in fixed commas, which is actually the binary representation of integers, is not always sufficient to implement very large or very small values. Values with a limited amount of significant figures. For this reason, computer scientists have imagined a coding whose significant part of the value is represented by a sequence of bytes and the position of the comma, by another byte meaning the offset to be applied to find the original position of the comma.

The significant part of the value is called MANTISSE.

The comma offset is specified by the EXPOSANT.

Depending on the desired precision the mantisse can be represented on 3 or 7 bytes. The exhibitor is always represented on a byte. When a value is expressed by a mantisse on 3 bytes plus the exponent, it is said that it is a real short or real 4 (in the jargon of computer scientists). If the mantisse has 7 bytes plus the exponent, it is said to be a real long or real 8.

At the accumulator level, the sign of the real is expressed by a specific byte ($FF = negative, 99 = positive). In memory the sign is determined by the strong weight bit of the mantisse (1 = negative, ÿ = positive).

The direction of the shift, characterising the lower reals, equal to or greater than the unit, is specified by the overall sign of the exponent. Indeed, rather than representing the exponent in the form of a value in addition to two, it is conventional to take as zero power the mean + 1 value of the potential offset range, i.e. 128. Thus when the exhibitor recorded in memory is 128 the value of the exhibitor used in calculating the number is equal to Zero.

187


===== Page 185 =====
The numbers represented as floating point are thus expressed as:
. F-17278
N-Sxeitx? With: S for the sign

M for the mantisse E for the exhibitor

Example to express 67 in decimal we will have: Exhibitor = $87 = 135T Real Exhibitor = 135 — 128 = 7 d where 7 offsets to the right to apply on the mantisse.

Mantisse = $86 49 49 soit en binaire 10000110 00000000 00000000
Conventionally the comma is set to the left of the binary value.

10000110 00000000 0000000
Apply the 7 offsets to the right, which amounts to multiplying the mantisse by 2 power 7(128)

190001 1,0 00000000 000000

WHOLE FRACTIONAL PART

In the floating point representation, the mantisse represents the fractional part of a fictitious real squared on the first significant digit. The exposure value indicating the value and direction of the offset achieved. This representation is called NORMALISEE and gives the best precision. As a result, any normalized float must have the strong weight bit of the mantisse at 1.

It is this characteristic that is used to indicate the sign of a floating number in memory. Indeed, in this case, the strong weight bit is implied to one and its physical location is used to specify the sign.

1 = Negative ÿ = Positive

This way of representing floating in memory makes a

byte compared to battery level representation
FAC ou ARG.

MO6 Monitor Page 4

1 INTRODUCTION
1.1 The M06 monitor

The MO6 monitor consists of different "routines" (programmes) each with a specific function: screen management, keyboard, optical pencil reading, game controllers, music generation, communication interface management, program player/recorder, etc.

ROM application programs, such as the Basic Resident or Assembly, use these routines for their own needs and you can do the same. The principle is:

(1) You load with values having a precise meaning some records of 6809, and possibly some records in RAM (words-memory on one or two bytes): these are input parameters.

2) You call the monitor routine performing the requested operation, using meoanism explained at $1.2

3) If applicable, you will recover the result of the operation from some of the 6809 records, and/or from RAM records: these are the return settings.

In some cases, there are no input parameters, or no return parameters, but the general schema is always the same.

In the following, some numbers or memoir addresses will be written in hexadecimal for more oommodity: in this case they will be followed by the letter "H", unless otherwise stated, any number not followed by a "H" is to be read in decimal,

Exemple: 10H = 16, 16H = 22, etc...

The two-byte RAM records will be noted as follows: XXXXH-XXXXH where X represents a hexadecimal digit. For a better understanding, we have given names to some memory registers: each one corresponds the address in memory of the first byte of the register.

Example: setting 4BOH in the 205AH-205BH register means: setting 04 in the 205AH address word memory and setting BOH in the address word memory
205BH.

The binary numbers will be preceded by the symbol "%", Bi bits are numbered from right to left, the smallest bit weight being bit 0, and the strongest bit weight is bit 7.


===== Page 194 =====
The M06 monitor

Exemple: %11001100
b7 = 1

b0O = 0

1.2 Access to monitor routines

The normalization of a monitor routine is done using the "SWI" instruction followed by a code (on one byte). These oodes are 66, divided into two groups differentiated by the state of bit 7, the 7 bits of low weight forming the code itself of the routine to which one wants to acede. Bit 7 has the following meaning: if it is 0, a "JSR" in the routine is simulated, i.e. the return is to the instruction following the call; if it is 1, a "JMP" is simulated, i.e. the return will be at the instruction following the call of the program where the call to the monitor is located.

EXAMPLE
PROG1 EQU bd
SWI
FCB 08H

Next instruction 1

The sound beep will be heard, then "next instruction 1" will be executed.

But if we meet in a program:

PROG1 EQU #
JSR PROG2
Next instruction 1

PROG2 EQU +
SWI
FCB 88H

Next instruction 2

The sound beep will be heard, then "next instruction 1" will be executed, not "next instruction 2".

The SWI instruction saves all records of 6809. Upon return, all records are returned in the state they had at the time of the call, except for the code-coded record of 6809 and of course the records to be returned (most often B, X and Y).

It is OBLIGATORY to call monitor routines using standardized access, Indeed, these routines can only correlate with the 6809 battery pointer and records in a very precise state,

Page 5


===== Page 195 =====
The M06 monitor Page 6

The list of monitor routine codes is summarized in
le § 19,2, ,

2 GESTION DES INTERRUPTIONS

The IRQ and FIRQ interruptions of the 6809 are programmable, i.e. that in ohacune orresponds to a RAM register containing the address of the program that has to process them, when energized or after a restart "at ohaud", these registers were initialized with the address of a monitor program,

The monitor uses IRQ (50 Hertz) interruptions to manage cursor flashing, mouse and keyboard repetition. These interruptions can be referred to your own programs by putting in the 2063H registry any non-zero value, and by placing in the TIMEPT (2061H-2062H) registry the address of your treatment program.

To manage FIRQs, you must put your program address in FIRQPT (2067H-2068H).

To manage IRQs, you must put your program address in IRQPT (2064H-2065H).

Your interruption program must end with an ITR.

To recover SWIs, you have to save the SWI1PT pointer (205EH-205FH) and store the address of your processing there, then if the SWI does not concern you, you have to finish by going to the old routine, without having changed the various registers,

Note however: some monitor routines are interruptible, and your programs should not change their settings,

Page 7


===== Page 197 =====
MO6 monitor

3 INITIALISATION

When energizing or after a "reset", the monitor executes a number of tests and initiations.

The hot or cold start test shall be carried out so as not to change in case of restart:

- setting of the optical rayon - olivier latency In both cases:

Interruption switch registers are initialized with the addresses of the corresponding monitor routines,

The registers containing the addresses of the various tables (Olavier decoding table, Standard Character Generator, User Character Generator) are reset to point to the standard tables.

If the mouse is recognized, it is selected if the optical pencil has not been applied for.

The tape is set to 2400 bauds.

The interruptions are opened and the cursor is off, the first Ram bank is selected,

The bits of the CONFIG (2079H) register are positioned.

The color palette is initialized at cold start, Hot, aon oontenue is preaerved.

All other registries are reset to standard values, usually zero.

4.1 Standard Alphabet GO

The GO caraotere generator is a suite of displayable oaractres, corresponding to the ASCII standard.

Each character consists of 8 ootets, which form a matrix of 6x8 points representing character, The first oaracter oorresponds to the lower line of the matrix.

Example: matrix defining "A"

00000000
O1 000O
0000-00
O000-00-0
000-00-0
0000-00
One thousand
00000000

In the generator, A will therefore be defined by the list of the following bytes: OOH, 42H, 42H, 7EH, 42H, 24H, 18H, OOH

In MO6, the starting address of the GO generator is displayed in RAM in the GENPTR (2073H-2074H). You can redefine the standard alphabet by creating your own generator according to the principle stated above, and then loading the GENPTR (2073H-2074H) register with the address of this generator. This generator ommence by describing the character 20H and ends with the character 7FH.

In the QWERTY version, the 60H character represents the book Sterling.

4.2. Alphabet G2

The G2 alphabet contains 6 characters, including acute, severe and circumflex accents, trema, cedilla and degree. These characters are defined according to the principle described above, and the accents are intended Being combined with tiny characters, This alphabet follows the previous alphabet.

4.3 User parameters

In addition to the register containing the address of the standard generator, a USERAF register (2070H-2071H) is intended to provide the starting address of a user ear generator. This generator will be organized as explained above, 8 bytes being neoessary to define a shell. As for the oodes, they

5 PRIMITIVES DE GESTION D'ECRAN
5.1 The 40 column mode

The MO6 can handle a full-page display of 320x200 points, with 16 shape colours, 16 background colors, and 16 tower colours chosen from 4096. In MO5 ompatible alphanumeric mode, it can display 25 lines of 40 characters, defined by a matrix of 8x8 points, in a color of choice of 16, regardless of the background color. In graphical mode, the central unit controls 8000 segments of 8 points each, and each segment corresponds to two bytes, located at the same logical address. One is located in memory called "FORM", the other in memory called "COULEUR", a bit of PIA allowing to physically address one or the other memory.

In a segment, a point can take two colors in ohoix: - a so-called "shape" oas the corresponding bit in the FORM memory byte is 1, and a so-called "bottom" bit, to which oas the corresponding FORM memory bit is 0. The byte at the same address but in memory COLOUR completes the information: the 4 bits of low weight give the color of the background, i.e. the colour to be taken by the points set at 0, and the 4 bits of strong weight give the color of the shape, i.e. the colour to be taken by the points set at 1. The 4 bits giving the color are pBVR ot p is the pastel bit (clear colors, except for orange) and where B, V, R indicate the presence of blue, green, or red (the three basic colors for T.V.) in the color.

It is sometimes imprecise to speak of "lighted" or "extracted" points: it is possible to have olair points belonging to the bottom (coding dono 0) and dark points belonging to the shape (coding dono 1).

For the record, please know that:

Red + green = yellow

Red + blue = magenta

Blue + Green = cyan

Red + green + blue = white

And of course, neither red nor blue nor green gives black.


===== Page 201 =====
The M06 monitor Page 12

Example: xxxx0000

R = 0: no red

V = 0: no green B = 0: no blue p = 0: dark color

The result is a black background,

Other example: 1111xxxx R = 1: red present V = 1: green present

B = 1: blue present

p 1: olar colour

The result should be a "blano olar" form. This colour duplicates the blano, an exception was made: it is orange.

5.2 The 80 column mode

In this mode, the M06 has 80 oolons of caraoteres and therefore increases to 640x200 points for the graph. On the other hand, it has only 2 colours, one for the shape, the other for the background. These two colors are numbers 0 and 1. In this case the FORM memory contains the bits of the oaraotere points of even columns, while the COLOR memory contains odd columns.

5.3. Bitmap mode 4 oouleurs

In this mode, the M06 still has 40 columns of Oaraoteres, thus 320 points wide, but only has 4 colors. However, color chaoun is independent of

his neighbor, So we have a screen page without stiffness in the 8-point segments.

For ohaque point of the screen, two bits code its color. Each of these bits is in a screen memory plan. The FORM plan contains the number 1 bit and the COLOR plan contains the number 0 bit. With the help of these 2 bits it is possible to oder 4 colors which are the first 4 colors of the palette.


===== Page 202 =====
The M06 monitor Page 13

The point in 0.0 (0000H) is oodized in bits 7 of the first byte of screen memories. The next point is encoded in bits 6, and so on until 319,199 encoded in bits 0 of the bytes in 1F3FH.

5.4 Pagel, Page2 and Overlay Modes

In this mode identical to the 40 oolons mode, only 2 colors of which a transparent are usable in ohaque page. However it offers you 2 separate screen pages. During the affiohage of one page, the other can be selected and then displayed by simple switching. Overlay mode allows you to overlay both pages by continuing to write in either.

Page 1 works in the FORM memory and ohaque bit a 1 represents the color 1, ohaque bit to 0, color 0,

a

Page 2 works in the memory COLOR and ohaque bit 4 1 represents the colour 2, ohaque bit to 0, the colour 0,

In overlay mode, page 1 is superimposed on page 2.

5.5. Bitmap mode 16 colors

This mode offers a resolution of 160x200 points out of 16 colors without rigidity. Each point on the screen is therefore coded on 4 bits,

By group of 4 pixels, we find the following organization:

P.O: coded on bits 7.6,5.4 of the byte of the FORME P.1 plane: coded on bits 3.2,1.0 of the ootet of the FORME P.2 plane: coded on bits 7.6,5.4 of the ootet of the COLOR P.3 plane: coded on bits 3.2,1.0 of the ootet of the COLOR The first 4 pixels are oodized in ootets 0

(OOOOH address) of the screen memory, while the last four are encoded in 1F3FH.

5.6 Triple overlay mode

In a mode similar to the overlay mode, 4 pages are superimposed with a priority to the display, but only a resolution of 160x200 points is available on the screen.


===== Page 203 =====
The M06 monitor Page 14

On display, Plan 1 is given priority on Plan 2, which is given priority on Plan 3, itself on Plan 4.

As with the 16 color bitmap, 4 pixels are coded in the 2 ootets of the same screen memory address.

The 7.6.5.4 bita of the FORME plan represent four main points of Plan 1.

The 3.2.1.0 bits of the FORME plane represent four consecutive points of plane 2.

The 7.6.5.4 bits of the COLOR plane represent four oonsecutive points of plane 2

The 3.2.1.0 bits of the COLOR plane represent four consecutive points of plane 4.

The background color of all planes is the logical color 0, and the respective colours of the shape of each plane (from first to background) are logical numbers 1,2,4 and 8 respeotively.

5.7 Incrustation

The M06 also offers the possibility of inorutation of the T.V. image in the image: if your M06 is equipped with the inrustation card, the "inorutation" mode (explained in § 5.11.4) will let appear the T.V. image at the plao of all inrustible oouleurs (see § 17), as if the colors became transparent.

The MO5 can only inorust on its black eye.

6 CLAVIER

6.1 Quick leoture of keyboard # input point code: OCH for JSR, 8CH for JMP. #* Return settings: - registers 6809 A, B and CC

This routine performs a quick reading of the keyboard, to test whether a key has been pressed or not. If no key has been pressed, B contains the value 3AH at the bit Z of the CC register is set to 1; Otherwise it is set to 0. In this case, B contains the key code pressed, and the status of bits 0, 1 and 2 of A indicates whether the SHIFT, BASIC, or CNT key was pressed:

- bit0: BASIC key - bit1: CNT key - bit2: SHIFT key

If the bit corresponding to the key is 1, the key has been pressed, otherwise the bit is 0.

6.2 Keyboard decode # input code: OAH for JSR, SAH for JMP. # Input settings:

- CHRPTR registers (206DH-206EH), STATUS (2019H), LATCLV
(2076H)

# Return settings: - registers 6809 B and CC

This routine decodes the keyboard, and manages the repetition of a key after a certain delay. This delay is programmable via the LATCLV register: by changing the contents of this register, you change the latency of the keyboard. Similarly, keyboard repetition can be inhibited by loading this register with a sufficiently high value. The sound beep that makes itself heard every time a key is hit can also be inhibited, in two ways: the first, classic, is to lower the sound of your T.V. receiver, the second to force to 1 bit 3 of the STATUS register.

B returns either the ASCII code of the character or the numeric code of the key (in case the BASIC key has been pressed). If no keys were pressed, or if one of the three BASIC, SHIFT, or CNT keys was pressed alone, or if several keys among BASIC, SHIFT, or CNT keys were pressed


===== Page 213 =====
The M06 monitor Page 24

Pressed simultaneously, B returns the zero value.

When the SHIFT LOCK key is turned off, the SHIFT key sets the caraotere printed in green on the key and the tiny ones. When switched on, SHIFT selects the green printed caraoteras and characters associated with the numbers; On the other hand, the ohiffles are accessible directly.

The CNT key forces the character printed in black on the hood to 0 bit 6 of the ASCII code. The BASIC key returns the numeric code of the key (depending on the keyboard mating), tcut by forcing the bit 7 to 1 (scured from 80H to B9H).

Access to tiny accents usually requires three supposed strikes:

- 1° stroke: accent key. - 2° stroke: key representing the accent.

- Three degrees hit: tiny letter.

However on the M06, access to some tiny accents commonly used in English can be made in one strike: e, è, ¢, à, ù.

Similarly, the accent may not be preceded by the ACC key; In this case, two strikes are sufficient.

In the QWERTY version, characters A, 0, U uppercase or lowercase with a trema can be cbtenated in one strike.

But in any case, you will have to make calls following this routine:

- the first call repeats in B the ACC code, i.e. 16 hours.

- the second call returns in B the code of the accent or Ila cedille.

- the third call goes back to the tiny one.

In the case of degree, only ACC (16H) and 30H are returned: two calls are sufficient.

The MO6 has 10 keys of fencticns. Codes searched by these wind keys from COH to C9H.

The ASCII character code is read in a table whose address is in the CHRPTR register. If you want to redefine your keyboard, 11 simply put in this register the address of the table containing your own ASCII ces, but the "accent" sequences can give unpredictable results ...

See the list of returned values when the BASIC key is locked simultaneously with a key.

7 PRIMITIVES GRAPHIQUES

7.1 Ignition or extinction of a point # input point code: 10H for a JSR, 90H for a JMP ® input parameters: - registers 6809 X and Y

7-1-1 Graphic mode

For all affichsge modes the way to pass the parameters is identical ,i1 should only be careful not to exceed the maximum number of points displayed horizontally in the chosen mode (320 points in 40 columns, Pagel, Page2, Overlay, Bitmap 4 colors; 640 points in 80 columns; 160 points in 16 colors Bitmap and Triple cverlay).

This routine colourifies the coordinates of X and Y, ot xX C <0.319 or 639 or 159> and Y C <0.199>. The colour of the point is determined by the contents of the FORME (2029H), which must be between -16 and +15.

In 40 column mode, if the color code is negative, the dot will be written in the background, i.e. the bit orresponding in the FORM memory byte will be set to zero. If the code is positive, the point will be written in form, i.e. the corresponding bit in the FORME memory byte will be set to 1. If the STATUS registry bit 4 (2019H) is at 1, only the FORM memory bit will be changed, the color will not be changed.

In Pagel mode, Page2, Overlay and Triple overlay, a positive code written in the color of the selected page, a negative set written in background color.

In 4-color Bitmap mode, only the 4 positive codes of 0 to 3 are usable; Only the form is written, the background has no meaning.

In 80 column mode, a positive code written in form, a negative number written in background.

. In 16 colour Bitmap mode, only 16 positive cedes from 0 to 15 can be used; Only the form is written, the background has no meaning.


===== Page 216 =====
The M06 monitor Page 27

A background color code is deduced from a form color code by adding 1 and taking the opposite.

The CHDRAW (2036H) register must be set to zero, otherwise it is in character mode, explained below.

Character mode

Only M05, 80 columns, Pagel, Page2, Overlay, Bitmap 4 colors are processed. This routine should not be accessed in the 16 colour Bitmap and Triple overlay modes.

If the contents of the CHDRAW (2036H) register are non-zero, it is assumed to contain the ASCII code of a "core point" to be displayed at the location defined by the X and Y registers. In this case, X C < 1,40 or 80> and Y C < 0,24> shall be used.

The colour of the "point character" is also provided by the FORME (2029H) register, but by the COLOUR (202BH), which contains the current background and shape colours.

The character of the CHDRAW (2036H) register can be accessed directly by calling CHPL$ (12H for JSR and 92H for JMP).

In both modes, the X and Y registers will be copied respitefully into the PLOTX (2032H-2033H) and PLOTY (2034H-2035H) registers that keep the abscisse and the order of the last item processed.

15 GESTION DU LECTEUR-ENREGISTREUR DE PROGRAMMES (LEP)
15.1 Reading and Writing the Band

# port of entry code: 20H for JSR, AOH for JMP

# input parameters;

- registers 6809 A, B and Y

- K7DATA (2040H), K7LENG (2041H) and CONFIG (2079H) registers

# Return settings:

- records 6809 A and B.

This routine allows reading or writing blocks of 253 bytes of data to the maximum. In reading an automatic recognition of the recording speed is performed. In writing CONFIG bit 2 (2079H) defines the speed: 0 = 2400 bauds, 1 = 1200 bauds.

These blocks may be of three types, the type being coded as follows:

- 00: file containing block. - 01: data block. - FFH: end of file bloo.

If A is non-zero, the routine erases a reading of the magnetic tape. It must contain the address of the memory area where the bloo read will be stored. On return, B will contain the block type, and A the "ohecksum" calculated from the data read (sum of data and checksum read). At the address indicated by Y, we will find:

- a byte giving the length of the block.

- n data bytes.

- a byte giving the "checksum" read.

If A = 0, this is a writing. B must contain the block type, and Y must have the address of the memory area where it is

find the data to write on the tape. This area shall have the following structure:


===== Page 229 =====
The MO6 Page monitor

1° byte: block length = number of data bytes +2.

2° byte: first given.

A (block length - 2) byte data: (n-1)° given.

n+1° byte: checksum control (unlike the sum of the data if you want to read validly have a checksum read that is null).

15.2 Engine: on/off # input point code: 22H for JSR, A2H for JMP # input parameter: - register 6809 A # return parameter: - Register 6809 CC

If the bit 0 of A is zero, the engine shall be shut down after half a second.

If bit 0 and bit 1 of A are each at 1, the engine is

fa

Started, and then there is a second delay.

If the bit 0 of A is at 1, and the bit 1 of A is at zero, the engine is started but there is no wait then.

Reliability will be increased if you follow the following advice:

# Start the engine for writing: start the engine, then wait a second, to make sure the engine speed stabilized before writing on the tape. A must then contain the value @xxxxxx11.

# Start the engine for a read: start the engine, no wait. A must then contain the value ZxxxxxxO 1.

# Engine shutdown after reading or writing: wait half a second to make sure that no part of the tape containing data will be under the cabestan, then shut down the engine. A shall then contain the value %xxxxxx10.

16 CONTROLEUR DE DISQUETTES

You can manage disk inputs/outputs on two levels: at the physical level using the monitor's entry point, or at a logical level by manipulating files in Basic Microsoft(R) format.

The diskettes are divided into 25 (QDD), 40 (lector 5"1/4) or 80 (lector 3"1/2) tracks of 16 sectors ohacune. A sector itself contains 128 bytes in single density or QDD, or 256 in double density.

In addition, the dual density controller can operate in both modes: single or double density. When starting, its working density must be selected after the controller initialization.

16.1 Physical disk inputs/outs # input point code: 26H for JSR, A6H for JMP # formatting code: 2AH for JSR, AAH for JMP # input parameters:

- DK.OPC (2048H), DK.DRV (2049H), DK.SEC (204CH),
DK,.TRK (204AH-204BH), DK.BUF (204FH-2050H)

# return settings: - register 6809 CC - register DK.STA (204EH)

In the event of an error during an operation the CC holding bit is forced to 1, otherwise it is forced to 0.

The DK.OPC register contains the transaction code to be performed:

- Code 00: must be set for formatting without verification.

- Code 01: requests the initialization of the controller. In this case, if the initialization could have taken place without errors, the retention bit of the condition code (CC) register is set to 0, and the type of controller "D" is returned to the DK. STA register. Otherwise, the hold-down bit is set to 1, and the 40H error code is set to the DK.STA registry. (see error codes below).

- Code 02: reading a sector, Possible error codes are 02, Where, 08, 10h. If an error has occurred, its code is returned to the DK.STA registry.


===== Page 231 =====
The M06 monitor Page 42

- Code 04: Transfer of controller in single density, no error possible.

Code 08: Writing a sector. The possible error codes are 01, 02, O4, 08, 10H, 20H. If an error has occurred, its code is returned to the DK.STA registry.

- code 10H: Double density controller switch, no error possible.

- Code 20H: search for the zero track. The possible error code is 10 hours.

- 40H code: search the track whose number is given by the DK.TRK register. The possible error code is 10 hours.

- Code 80H: option of checking in writing. We have to make a logical "OR" between this code and the code of the operation we want to verify. The returned error codes are the same as those of the specified operation plus code 20H.

Parameters to pass following the requested operation:

# DK.DRV register (2049H): This register must contain the number of the sounded player, i.e. a value between 0 and 3. The numbers 0 and 1 correspond to the two faoes of the internal drive and the numbers 2 and 3 on both sides of the external drive. Single density drives can be 4.

# DK.TRK register (204AH-204BH): this record must contain the runway number, with runways numbered from 0 to 24, 39 or 79.

# DK.SEC register (204CH): This register must contain the sector number where you want to read or write. This number must be between 1 and 16.

The DK. NUM register (204DH): this register contains the interlacing of logical sectors on the diskette during formatting. This allows faster access speeds to the disc. On MO6, the interface used by Basic is 7. Fast sector reading can be optimal with a 2.

# DK.BUF register (204FH-2050H): This register must contain the beginning address of a 128-byte single density RAM buffer zone, or 256-byte double density buffer zone, either to read the data to be written on disk or to write the data read on disk.

*® DK.STA register (204EH): this register contains the error code, or controller type after correct initialization,


===== Page 232 =====
The M06 monitor Page 43

Here are the various possible errors:

- code O1: protected diskette; This error can only appear after a written request.

- code 02: there are timing problems or data have been lost.

- code 04: sector error; the sector identifier is incorrect (sector cannot be read or error on the checksum), however the track can be correlated.

- code 08: data error; the sector identifier is correct, but the data cannot be read, or the checksum is inoorrect.

- code 10H: non-pret reader; the engine is not en route, or the specified drive is non-existent.

- code 20H: error on verification; the buffer zone in

memory and the corresponding area written on the diskette are not identical.

16.2 Basic Microsoft(R) format

To ensure compatibility between the various applications, the files created by the M06 follow the Basic Microsoft(R) standard.

Runway 20 is a reserved area to report the status of the diskette. It shall be organised as follows:

# sector 1: reserved # sector 2: file allocation table or "FAT"

# sectors 3 to 16: catalogue

16.2.1 The file allocation table

The files are organized into blocks of 1K bytes in single density, or 2K ootets in double density. So we've got two bloos per track. Blocks are numbered from 0. Each byte of the fiohier allocation table, from byte 1, represents a physical bleo.


===== Page 233 =====
The monitor of the M06 Page

Organization of the FAT:

# Jctet 0: OQ.

# Octet 1: Block 0, Track 0, Sectors 1 to 8. # Octet 2: Block 1, Track 0, Sectors 9 to 16. # Octet 3: Block 2, Track 1, Sectors 1 to 8. # Octet 4: Block 3, Track 1, Sectors 9 to 16.

eeeemeeneeeepaeeseSstsHPeEHtHFHFFeHHHHHHHEHEHSHEHKHEHHE HHHH HHS

# Octet 2d-1: block 2d-2, track j-1, sectors 1 to 8. # Octet 2d: block 2d-1, track j-1, sectors 9 to 16.

# Octet 160: Block 159, Runway 79, Sectors 9-16.

Note: In single density the FAT is limited to 127 blocks.

A "FAT" byte representing a physical block may have as values:

® FFH, which means block not allocated. ® FEH, which means reserved block.

# Any number from 0 to BFH, means allocated block. In this case, the number represents the next logical block number of the same file.

# Any number of C1H to C8H, means last block of a file, The 4 bits of low weight indicate the number of sectors used in this last block.

16.2.2 The catalogue

The catalogue lists the files and covers 14 sectors. Each file is listed on 32 bytes. Ilya done 4 files listed per sector in single density, and 8 files per sector in double density. In total, the catalogue can therefore list 56 files in single density, and 112 in double density.

44


===== Page 234 =====
The M06 monitor Page 45

Each file is listed as follows:

Octets 00 to 07: File name, oadré to the left, completed with blanks.

Octets 08 to OAH: File suffix (.BAS, .BIN, etc...), oadré à Gauohe, omplated by whites,

Octet OBH: File type: 0 for a Basic ASCII or binary program, 1 for Basic ASCII data, 2 for a machine language (binar) program, 3 for an assembly fiochier published in ASCII.

OCH Octet: Semaphore: FFH for ASCII, 00 for binary.

ODH Octet: Number of the first logical block of the file.

OEH-0FH: Number of bytes used in the last sector of the file.

Octets 10H-17H: Comment associated with file. Octets 18H-1AH: Date: dd-mm-yy

Bytes 1BH-1FH: Reserved,

The first byte of each entry in the catalogue indicates its state:

# 00H: unallocated entry, no file listed for this entry,

# 20H-7FH: ASCII code of the first character of the file name, assigned input,

# FFH: logical end of the catalogue.

These bytes are placed at FFH, each time a flyer is created, the logical end of the catalogue is moved in the first byte of the next entry, until the catalogue is full. When a file is destroyed, the first byte of its entry is set to zero (unallocated entry). In this case, any newly created file will be given priority over its input.

17 PROGRAMMATION DE LA PALETTE
# input point code: 3CH for JSR, BCH for JMP # input parameters: - records 6809 A, X and Y # output parameter: - register 6809 X

This routine allows you to define the value of each of the 16 colors displayed among a palette of 4996 colors.

The colors are defined by the three components Red, Green and Blue, as well as a bit that defines if the color is transparent in inlay. The coding is as follows:

XXxMBBBB VVVVRRRR coded on 2 bytes,

where M represents transparency in inerustation (1: opaque, 0: transparent), BBBB coding blue, VVVV coding green and RRR coding red. Each component is encoded over 4 bits, so it can done 16 values. Three components allow to have 16x16x16, i.e. 4096 colors,

17.1 Reading or writing a box in the palette

The number of the color you want to change is placed in Register A.

Register X is a ET mask. It allows you to choose the bits you want to change. The 0 bits will be changed, those to 1 kept.

The Y register is a OR mask. It allows setting the state of the bits that are 0, or that are set to 0 by the ET mask,

In output, X contains the value rowed in the palette. The operation is: Palette = (palette ET X) OR Y.

Note: This operation is performed with line return waiting.

Example: if you want to put the green at half intensity without touching the other components for color 2, you will charge:

A with 2
X avec $111 1 1111 0000 1111 (FFOFH)
Y avec £000 0 0000 0111 0000 (0070H)


===== Page 236 =====
The M06 monitor Page 47

Output X contains the value of box 2 of the palette.

Example: if we want to put the color 5 transparent for iacrustation, we load

A with 5

X avec #111 0 1111 1111 1111 (EFFFH)

Y avec £000 0 0000 0000 0000 (0000H)
Output X contains the value of box 5 of the pallet. Example: to read the contents of a box without the

modify, 11 is obvious that X must be OFFFH and Y to 0. The result is in X.

17.2 Programmen complete palette

Register A in this case must contain OFFH. The X register must show a 32-byte table that contains the 16 values of the 16 ooded oouleurs as described earlier.

The first 2 bytes smell the color 0, while the last 2 code the color 15.

Note: the operation is carried out with waiting for weft return,

17-3 Colors used by display mode

In 40 oolons mode and 16 colors Bitmap the 16 colors are accessible.

In mede 80 columns the background color is the number 0, the shape of the number 1.

In 4-color Bitmap mode, O to 3 colors are used.

In Pagel, Page2 and Triple Overlay, 0a 2 oouleurs are used.

In Triple overlay mode, 0 to 4 oouleurs are used.

In all modes the 16 colors are available for the tour.

17.4 Content of PALETTE.CFG file

The PALETTE.CFG file contains the data from a palette saved by the utility "PALETTE RULING".


===== Page 237 =====
The M06 monitor Page 48

This is a binary file of 32 bytes (16 words of 16 bits) describing the 16 colors of the palette from color 0 to color 15.

Each word has the following format
XXXMBBBB VVVRRRR
M represents the bit of incrustation. BBBB is the quantity of blue VVVV is the quantity of green

RRRR is the amount of red. x not significant

It is also possible to switch a RAM bank in

cartridge

to run programs there, To switch a bank in the given space or cartridge space, see the documentation of the gate mode page.

Standard monitor entry points

The following codes are 7-bit codes. The bit 7 is set to zero to simulate a JSR, to 1 to simulate one The others are reserved for the internal use of the monitor.

00 : Back to the menu.

02 : Display of a shell,

Where: Color storage,

06 : Formatting.

08: Issue of a sound beep.

OAH : Keyboard reading,

OCH: Quick reading of the keyboard.

OEH : Traced from a right segment.

10H: Ignition or extinction of a point. 12H: Writing a "character" point, 14H: Reading the color of a point. 16h : Reading the optical pencil button. 6pm: Reading the optical pencil.

1AH: Reading the Eran.

1CH : Play game controllers.

1EH: Generation of music.

8pm: Reading/writing on the tape. 22H: Engine start/stop. 24H: Parallel interface management. 26H: Diskette controller.

24H: Disk formatting,

3CH: Programming the palette.

3ER: Reading the mouse buttons.

HOH : Reading the mouse.

WOH: Management of RS232.

20 PRISE DE LA SOURIS

At the MO6, the mouse is connected to the game 0 socket. Let's name by I any entry on the game socket and by 0 any exit to the mouse. The catch pattern is as follows:

mm directions
15 y 3 2 1 ! > X+
! 9 8 7 6 ! !
mms v Y+ Pin 1:I Range 1 XB/YB = me Pin 2:I Range 2 Pin 3:I XB XA/YA rs wee ro Pin 4:I YB Pin 5:0 (+5v) sense(+) sense(-) Pin 6:I YA Pin 7:I XA Pin 8.0 Pull down 20 Ohms. Pin 9:0 (Gnd)

Analysis: Any released trigger returns a level "1". During operation the mouse provides 2 quadrature signals for each direction X and Y. The frequency depends on the speed of rotation of the ball. The software must test the variations in the level of outputs A and B, deduce the direction of rotation and increment or decrement the meters.

Both game sockets are connected to the PIA game according to the following assignments:

+5v PA3 PA2 PAT PAO +5v PA7 PA6 PA5 PAY
1 5 4 3 2 1! 1 5 l 3 2 1!
1 9 8 7 6 I 1 9 8 6 |!
GND PBO PB2 PB6 GND PB1 PB3 PB7
CAT CA2 Take 0 Take 1 The mouse must be connected to the oO game rise. By

elsewhere on each, each bit can be programmed to work as an input or output. So you can easily detect the type of connected device and adapt to it,

21 DIFFERENCES ENTRE LE MO5 ET LE MO6

This chapter summarizes the differences between M05 and M06. It repeats these differences both in the zero page and in the entry points and in the PIA and gate mode page. The various machines are differentiable in reading the FFFOH byte:

OQ: MOS
1 : M06

It is understood that a maohine serial number n supports all entry points of machines of 0 year.

21.1 Differences in ports of entry The initializations are made according to the machine, PUTC$ allows access to the various display modes. The G2 rifle generator has been extended to the degree. GETSC can read the degree. PLOT$ and DRAW$ manage MO6 modes.

KTST makes additional values for new touohes.

A closure of interruptions forbids the reading of the mouse,

GETS$ handles G2 characters.

SRCO$ is new: SR232 management.

GEPE$ and PEIN$ are new: mouse reading. SETP$ is new: palette programming,

The SWI2 statement calls the extramonitor,

21.2 Differences in Zero Page Records

STATUS: bit 5 gives the QWERTY/QWERTZ version on the export version,

RCMPODE (1 byte: graphic printer code) is replaced by SCRMOD (1 byte: display mode).

60


===== Page 250 =====
MO6 Monitor Page 61

SS

The reserved bytes from 2077H to 207EH are occupied by: CONFIG (1 byte: machine configuration).

XMOUSE (2 bytes: position of mouse in X).

YMOUSE (2 bytes: position of mouse in Y).

CPMUTE (1 byte: meter to silence the mouse),

The top of the system stack is reserved for the following registers

MA7E7 (1 byte: copy of gate A7E7H registry). RS.OPC (1 byte: RS232 command log). RS.STA (1 byte: RS232 status register),

MODE (1 byte: RS232 transmission type). VITESSE (1 byte: RS232 speed).

SAVTOUR (1 byte: saving the color of the spin).

The last 2 bytes of LPBUFF are recovered for the mouse:

DXMOUSE (1 byte: X relative mouse),

DYMOUSE (1 byte: relative mouse Y),

et le M06.

21.3.2 The PIA game

The PIA game is shared with the mouse and printer. See postings at $19.4.

21.3.3 The communication interface

The MO6 manages the new RS232 communication interface. It cannot handle the old interface that works on M05 systems. These systems cannot accept the new interface.

21.3.4 RAM and ROM banks

The MO6 is equipped with the gate mode page that allows complete management of RAM and ROM banks. See the documentation of the gate mode page to fully use this machine.

The MO6 Exmonitor

1 GENERALITES ET INITIALISATION
1.1 General principles

We can use EXTRAMON on any MO6. The call is made using the SWI2 instruction of microprocessor 6809. Simply use the SWI2 statement with Registry B containing the Veulue function number (see the function list in $10). Some precautions for use are essential

a/ Do not have any interruption sub-programs in the BOOOH-EFFFH space.

b/ Have remained EXTRAMON before anything with RESETC (cold) or RESETW (hot) .

e/ If the disc is used, the buffer zones should be indicated with FCBINI.

d/ It is important to think about catching up with the mistakes made by EXTRAMON. At the exit of any routine (unless you are very sure of yourself) you have to test register B, this one indicates an error code or zero if it all went well. Error codes correspond to Basic error code (see error code table),

e/ The input parameters pass through the 6809 records, while others pass through the ram in area 2100H-22FFH. The

Return parameters are all in ram. All records except B are preserved.

1.2 Initialization of EXTRAMON # point of entry: RESETC (00)

# Return settings

~ NBANK (218CH) Number of banks (still 6). - MODEL (227BH) Model Number

OOH on M06 ~ TYPDSK (2219H) Controller Type

OOH single density controller 80K 01H double density controller 51/4 02H double density controller 3"1/2 03H controller QDD 50K

The first call from EXTRAMON must be the call to RESETC. This routine will initialize the EXTRAMON memory area and perform a (non-destructive) RAM test. In return the NBANK variable will contain the number of ram banks present in the system. Drive density is initialized according to controller type, single density on a single density controller and double on a double controller. When starting hot, call RESETW (01)

Page 3


===== Page 255 =====
The M06 Exmonitor

2 LE GRAPHIQUE
2.1 General

Before calling the graphics routines, you must at least call the CHOICE routine once to choose your trace mode and set your work window. Most graphic routines work from the point (XXXXX,YYYYYY) which is the

graphic cursor. The graphic cursor is the center of the ellipses, the corner of the rectangles and dreites etc...

2.2. The work window

There is no call to set the work window, 11 simply modify the XL,YB and XR,YT registers (XLeft, YBottom, XRight, YTop). But be careful, there is no verification by EXTRAMON. It is absolutely necessary to initialize the window because there are no defaults and no graphics would be displayed.

XL (21A5H~21A6H) left margin.

YB (21A7H-21A8H) the high margin.

XR (21A9H-21AAH) right margin.

YT (21ABH-21ACH) the low margin.

These 4 16-bit registers are in pixels.

To work on the complete screen in 40 columns, 11 must
mettre : XL=0, XR=319, YB=0, YT=199.

2.3 Choosing the type of plot # painted entry: CHOICE (28)

# Input settings:

~ TRATYP (21A0H) Trace Type 0-3 - COLOR (219FH) Colour from -16 to +15 - WITH (2288H) Flag with or without colour

OOH ==> with the color FFH ==> without the color(form only)

EXTRAMON has initialized as many things as possible so that future graphic operations will see as quickly as possible.

. There are 4 screen types managed by EXTRAMON, these must be selected beforehand thanks to PUTC$ by an ESC sequence

Page 4


===== Page 256 =====
The MO6 Exmonitor

The plan 320x200, 16 colors, says MO5 mode

Plan 640x200, bicolor, says 80 colennes

The plan 320x200, 4 colors, says Bitmap 4 colors

The plan 160x200, 16 layers, says Bitmap 16 colors

Pagel, Page2 and Overlay modes are seen by EXTRAMON as Bitmap4. Triple Overlay mode is completely unmanaged.

Every time you want to change one of these settings,

You have to call the CHOICE routine. The pattern type must be in TRATYP, the color in COLOR.

TRATYP = O ==> normal route

TRATYP = 1 ==> transparent (or logical) TRATYP = 2 ==> inversion (or exclusive) TRATYP = 3 ==> mode "and"

In MO5 mode, negative colors will draw in background instead of shape. If WITH is at FFH only the FORME memory plan is affected.

In 4-color Bitmap mode, only the 2 bits of low COLOR weight are taken into account. The WITH byte at FFH means that we only write in the FORME plan (here RED plan).

In 80-column mode, only the COLOR sign will act on the plot (trace of 1 or 0). The WITH byte has no effect.

LS

The point is drawn only if it is inside the window.

Example:
LDX #100
LDY #50                  ; Point (100,50)
LDB #PSETXY              ; Call the PSETXY routine

2.6 The reason for filling

For any filling, a pattern must be provided. A pattern is a suite of 8 bytes with the same conventions as PUTC$ graphics. In MACP (227DH-227EH) a pointer should be provided to this pattern.

records 6809 X and Y FILFLG finish point (21EFH) OOH ==> empty rectangle

3 LES TORTUES
3.1 General

A turtle is a graphic object (iron wire) that can be moved, enlarged, deformed and passed. Generally speaking to a turtle by calling EXTRAMON, 11 simply choose the desired function in B (as usual) and provide a pointer to the turtle descriptor in the 6809 Y register.

This descriptor has a length of 14+n bytes, n depending on the complexity of the shape of the turtle. Most of the things you can do with a turtle are done through a call from EXTRAMON. The few exceptions will be dealt with in the final,

The first thing you need to do to use a turtle is to get it started. This is done with the INITORTUE routine.

The handling of these commands is very close to that of Basie 128,

3.2. Initialization # input point : INITORTUE (39) # input parameters : - register 6809 Y Point on an area of 27 bytes minimum

The area is initialized. The turtle is at the center of the screen, invisible, raised pencil, normal scale, zero direction and rotation, fast mode and in the shape of an isocele triangle (like the LOGO turtle).

3.3 Visibility # input point: SHOW (33) # input parameters

- register 6809 Y Point on the descriptor of the turtle - register 6809 A QOH ==> hides the turtle FFH ==> shows the turtle

To be able to easily erase and re-display, turtles are dissected in "or exclusive", the color of the turtle is the color chosen in CHOICE, In MO5 mode, the color is not put in reverse video, so a turtle can leave traces of colors, It is advisable to ask without the color (see the CHOICE routine), if you want to avoid this effect.


===== Page 266 =====
The M06 Exmonitor Page

3.4. Moving # Input Point: FWD (37) # Input Settings:

- register 6809 Y Point on turtle descriptor - register 6809 X Length -256 < XK < 256

This routine allows to move the turtle in its current direction, this corresponds to the ADVANCE command of the LOGO interpreter. The displacement takes place in a spherical virtual window from -32768 to 32767 in X as in Y. The visible part is defined by the viewing window.

3.5 Direction # point of entry: HEAD (34) # input parameters

- register 6809 Y Point on the descriptor of the turtle - register 6809 X Angle (signed) - register 6809 A OOH => The angle is absolute

The angle is relative

The positive sense is the clockwise direction. The angles are provided in 256th of a circle, the FWD will now go in this new direction. In relative terms, this corresponds to the LOGO interpreter's LAW instruction. In absolute terms, this corresponds to the FCAP order (fixed heading).

3.6 Rotation # input point: ROT (35) #* input parameters:

- Registry 6809 Y Crab descriptor tip - Register 6809 X Angle (signed) - register 6809 A OOH => The angle is absolute

The angle is relative

The positive direction is the direction of the needles of a m entree. The angles are provided in 256th of a circle. The shape of the turtle turns on itself from X 256th circle without changing direction.

3.7 Size

# point of entry : ZOOM (36)

# Input settings

register 6809 Y Point on turtle descriptor register 6809 X Expansion

Register 6809 A OOH => Enlargement is absolute FFH => Enlargement is relative

Enlargement is limited in absolute terms between 0 and 255. Be careful! It is not advisable to enlarge too much an object. EXTRAMON does not test anything for a matter of speed, but if a segment of an object exceeds 256 pixels, 11 can be incorrectly displayed.

3.8 Trace # Input Point: TRACE (31) # Input Settings:

- register 6809 Y Point on the descriptor of the turtle - register 6809 A OOH => Lift the FFH pencil => Lower the pencil

Each object is associated with a pencil, it allows drawing omme with LOGO. The route is in the current mode chosen with the CHOICE routine. This corresponds to the LOGO interpreter's LC and BC orders.

3.9 Speed # point of entry: ANIME (32) # input parameters

- register 6809 Y Point on the hare descriptor - register 6809 A OOH => Fast mode FFH => Slow mode

This allows to modulate the quality of the display by giving priority to the speed or monitoring of the display. In "slow" mode, the object is displayed again with each modification. While in "fast" mode, EXTRAMON only replays when moving the object, In particular, the actions of ZOOM and ROT are postponed to the next movement of the object. This allows to modify several parameters of the turtle quite quickly. It is also possible for speed issues, or more simply to move the turtle other than by FWD, to modify certain descriptor bytes directly. As the changes are made, you need to call MOVE to see the medifications on the screen. On

+

can thus in one call to EXTRAMON change the size, the

M06 Ext.

position, retating, etc. of the object.

3.10 Positioning # point of entry: MOVE (38) # input parameters - register 6809 Y Point on the descriptor of the turtle Here is the list of things that can be changed in the descriptor

TX (Y+6) abscisse of turtle (3 bytes) TY (Y+9) ordered of turtle (3 bytes) TROT (Y+12) rotation (1 byte)

TTAI (in Y+13) size (1 byte)

TDIR (in Y+14) direction (1 byte)

TX and TY are known at 256th pixel, the last ecctet representing the fractional part of pixel.

To change the shape of the turtle, the standard farm proposed by EXTRAMON must be modified. You have to ask the object to hide before changing its shape so as not to have strange effects, This form is in the descriptor from TFORME (Y+16). The shape of a turtle is a series of advance and turning pencil raised or lowered. The shape is therefore defined by a series of doublets or triplets. Here is an example:

TFORME FDB 0,15,0 LC AV 15 TD O (BC)
FDB 30,117 AV 30 TD 117
FDB 15,74 AV 15 TD 74
FDB 30,74 AV 30 TD 74
FDB 0,0

These are done in general doublets (LONGUEUR,ANGLE), but if the length is zero then the following doublet will be made raised pencil. Two zeros complete the description. The example is the default shape of turtles.

4 LES MATHEMATIQUES
4.1 General

This is a set of mathematical functions all as exciting as each other. With these you can add, multiply, divide and I pass, all on real short or long and signed 16-bit integers.

4.2 Single operations

The argument must be in the FCC accumulator (see below). So a simple call to SIN, COS etc... makes you the result (in FCC as well of course). The result type may be different from the start type, e.g. SIN(integer)=> real 4.

4.3 binary operations

The left argument must be in ARG and the right argument in FCC. Both arguments must be of the same type and the type in VALTYP. You find the expected result in FCC. Be careful, the type of result may not be that of the operands. For example 20,000+20000 sum of two signed whole vouchers cannot be a signed whole, so you will end up with a real in FCC; If you have any doubts, look at VALTYP. If you want the calculation to be done in double precision, VALTYP=8 and DBLFLG (2103H) should be set to $FF.

4.4 Description of Accumulator The main accumulator is called FCC, For a real short, we will have the following codings

The real shorts are coded (unless sign) on 4 bytes. The first byte is the exhibitor E, the other 3 the mantisse M.

Hey! Exposing-128 1! number:= O.Mantisse x 2!


===== Page 271 =====
M06 Ext.

Ex Number = 100 decimal.

Exposant = 87H = $10000111
Mantisse = C80000H = %$11001000 00000000 00000000

Note: For these routines, a null exponent implies that the number is equal to zero, whatever the mantisse.

When entering any routine, the number must be normalized (the strong weight bit of the mantisse must be at 1). On output, EXTRAMON renders standardized numbers. As long as you use EXTRAMON reutines for your calculations, this is transparent to you,

FCC's various cemposants include:

For a real short

FACEXP (214EH): binary exponent (from -128 +127) FACHO (214FH): 24 bit mantisse. strong weights FACMO (2150H): medium weight mantisse

FACLO (2151H): low weight mantisse

FACSGN (2156H): Sign of Accumulator

VALTYP (2105H): Accumulator type (at 4)

For a long real, it's the same except:
VALTYP (2105H) : à 8
(2152H-2155H): 4 bytes follow the mantisse

Fear a 16-bit integer: FACEXP,FACHO,FACSGN are useless FACMO,FACLO contain integer
VALTYP (2105H) : à 2

Here are the components of ARG:

ARG (2159H) is the 2nd battery, it will serve you for binary operations. It is very similar to FCC:

ARGEXP , ARGHO, ARGMO, ARGLO (2159H-215CH) for real 4 DARGHO (215DH-2160H) +4 ceets for real 8 ARGSGN (2161H) sign of the argument for real ARGMO, ARGLO (215BH-215CH) for integers.

In FACSGN and ARGSGN only bit 7 is used. This bit to 1 represents a negative number.

5 LE DOS

The DOS is compatible with MICROSOFT(R) format, and the description of the diskettes can be found in the MO6 monitor description.

CAUTION: In double density, the DOS uses only 255 ectets per sector (out of 256 available).

5.1 DOS Latinization # Entry Point: FCBINI (02) # Entry Settings:

- SECBUF (2197H) Point on a buffer for a sector. 256 or 128 bytes depending on density.

- FATPTR (2199H) Point free for 5 FATs. DSBLEN (166) bytes by FAT.

- register 6809 Y Number of discs you want to use (5 maximum).

- register 6809 A Max number of files that you want to open at the same time.

- register 6809 X Point free zone fear FCBLEN (283) x A bytes. Reserve the place for A File Control Bleck.

After starting EXTRAMON with a RESETC, you must initialize the EXTRAMON disk-related variables. It is necessary to specify where, how, when, etc... that is, the number of disks you want to be able to use, and the number of files you want to be able to collect at the same time. This is done by the FCBINI routine.

The disk density is chosen by EXTRAMON at startup, it works in single density on 80K single density controllers as well as on QDDs, and uses double density on all other controllers. There is no RAM drive management on the M06. There is, however, the pessibility of using a disk cache.

5.2 The disk cache # input point : INICACHE (70) # input parameters :

- ONOFF (22B0H) Flag with or without cache. - BLOCS (22AEH) Point on a table of blue descriptors.

For those who have not planned the cache, there is no one who wants an explicit demand. The flag ONOFF (22BO0OH) must be set to FFH, the BLOCS (22AEH) pointer must be


===== Page 276 =====
The M06 Exmonitor Page 25

address a table of cache descriptors. There is no limit to the number of caches, each descriptor has a length of 8 bytes divided as well

KADRESSE (2 bytes) KBANQUE buffer address (1 byte) Bank number Reserved (5 cctets)

You can see as many caches as you want, just initialize the first 3 ectets of each descriptor to a large enough memory zene to read a track of 25716 in double density and 129#16 in single. KBANQUE is a Cempris logical bank number between 1 and 6. EXTRAMON recognizes the end of the cache descriptor table when a zero is in the first position.

After updating your pointers, just call the INICACHE (70) routine to make it work. Don't forget to put ONOFF to FFH. If you want to make a check without a cache, just set ONOFF to zero the time required for your operation. For example, you can ban writing caches and autcrise them for reading only. For SAVE or LOAD-style operations, you can also ban disk cache because it does not take anything.

EXTRAMON assumes that the disc is not removed from the drive, if you want to warn it of a risk of change, you must weigh the flag RSKCHG (2189H) at FFH. EXTRAMON will then consider its caches incorrect and will reread the disc if necessary, In impert operatics like CLOSE, KILL, is... EXTRAMON emptys his caches.

The controller type is in the TYPDSK byte (2219H), so the programmer can request a cache on QDD and not on disk, if it prefers security to speed.

5.10 Backup of a floppy disk # input points: BACKUPO (10), BACKUP1 (11) #* input point: NEW-BACKUP (71) #* input parameters

- DK.DRV (2049H) Drive destination

- register 6809 A Drive source

- DEBZON (216BH-216CH) Working memory area - FINZON (216EH-216FH) End of this area

# Return settings

- SWPFLG (219DH) Flag: Should we swap?

5.10.1. Source reader different from destination drive

In this case DEBZON and FINZON must delimit a memcir zone from the axis the length of a track i.e. 2 cu 4 Ko depending on the density of the drive, otherwise an error is generated.

5.10.2. Secure reader = destination drive

This BACKUP uses all the memory, from Bank 1 to NBANK (218CH). You can always modify NBANK to preserve your memory. The backup will only take longer. The control is rendered to you after a change of diskette is required, it is up to you to display the necessary message and centinute the backup by calling BACKUP1. The veus SWPFLG flag indicates a SWAP request, If SWPFLG is null, it is that the operation is completed.

This entry point is very close to BACKUPO, the only difference is better memory management, The painters DEBZON and FINZON are then on 3 bytes, the 3rd being a bank number (between 1 and 6). DEBZON and FINZON define the area used in all cases. For further processing in the case of a single-reader backup, 11 just call BACKUP1.
